答对

基准介绍

OlympiadBench 收录国际/中国奥赛与高考的奥赛级数学、物理难题，中英双语、含部分图示，评测模型的高阶科学推理与长链计算能力。对应本仓库能力维度 D05（高阶学科推理）。

本次加载开放题（OE）配置，含纯文本（TO）与多模态（MM），排除需人工评阅的证明题（TP）。提示沿用官方 make_prompt，要求模型用 \boxed{} 给出最终答案。

判分使用官方符号判分器 AutoScoringJudge（sympy + LaTeX 解析，含数值容差与表达式/方程/区间等价），按学科、语言、模态、答案类型分桶统计。

主页：https://github.com/OpenBMB/OlympiadBench

题目示例

#OE_MM_maths_en_COMP-2231subject=Math language=en modality=MM answer_type=Numerical
The following is an open-ended problem from an International Math competition. The answer of The problem should be a numerical value. Please calculate the answer according to the given requirements and the information provided. Please use LaTeX format to represent the variables and formulas used in the solution process and results. Please end your solution with "So the final answer is \boxed{answer}." and give the result explicitly. Turbo the snail sits on a point on a circle with circumference 1. Given an infinite sequence of positive real numbers $c_{1}, c_{2}, c_{3}, \ldots$. Turbo successively crawls distances $c_{1}, c_{2}, c_{3}, \ldots$ around the circle, each time choosing to crawl either clockwise or counterclockwise. For example, if the sequence $c_{1}, c_{2}, c_{3}, \ldots$ is $0.4,0.6,0.3, \ldots$, then Turbo may start crawling as follows: <image_1> Determine the largest constant $C>0$ with the following property: for every sequence of positive real numbers $c_{1}, c_{2}, c_{3}, \ldots$ with $c_{i}<C$ for all $i$, Turbo can (after studying the sequence) ensure that there is some point on the circle that it will never visit or crawl across.
参考答案['$\\frac{1}{2}$']
#OE_MM_maths_en_COMP-2237subject=Math language=en modality=MM answer_type=Numerical
The following is an open-ended problem from an International Math competition. The answer of The problem should be a numerical value. Please calculate the answer according to the given requirements and the information provided. Please use LaTeX format to represent the variables and formulas used in the solution process and results. Please end your solution with "So the final answer is \boxed{answer}." and give the result explicitly. In the diagram, $\angle A B F=41^{\circ}, \angle C B F=59^{\circ}, D E$ is parallel to $B F$, and $E F=25$. If $A E=E C$, determine the length of $A E$, to 2 decimal places. <image_1>
参考答案['79.67']

Looking at this problem, I need to find the velocity of Adithya's rocket in Eddie's Rindler frame $S_2$ at the moment (defined by $S_2$'s simultaneity) when Adithya's proper time equals 0.75 s.

## Setting Up Coordinates

**Inertial frame $S_0$** (initially at rest with both rockets):

Adithya's worldline (proper acceleration $a_0$ to the right):
$$x_A = \frac{c^2}{a_0}\left(\cosh\frac{a_0 \tau_A}{c} - 1\right), \quad ct_A = \frac{c^2}{a_0}\sinh\frac{a_0 \tau_A}{c}$$

With $R_A = c^2/a_0 = 3 \times 10^8$ m and at $\tau_A = 0.75$ s, where $a_0 \tau_A/c = 0.75$:
- $x_A = 3 \times 10^8 \times 0.2947 = 8.84 \times 10^7$ m
- $ct_A = 3 \times 10^8 \times 0.8223 = 2.467 \times 10^8$ m

## Eddie's Rindler Frame $S_2$

With $a_E = a_0/2$ and $R_E = c^2/a_E = 6 \times 10^8$ m, the transformation is:
$$x_0 = R_E - (R_E - \sigma)\cosh\frac{a_E \tau}{c}, \quad ct_0 = (R_E - \sigma)\sinh\frac{a_E \tau}{c}$$

**Rindler time at event E** (where Adithya's clock = 0.75 s):
$$\tanh\frac{a_E \tau}{c} = \frac{ct_A}{R_E - x_A} = \frac{2.467 \times 10^8}{5.116 \times 10^8} = 0.4822$$

This gives $a_E \tau/c = 0.5258$, so $\tau = 1.0516$ s.

**Adithya's position in $S_2$:**
$$\sigma = R_E - \sqrt{(R_E - x_A)^2 - (ct_A)^2} = 6 \times 10^8 - 4.482 \times 10^8 = 1.518 \times 10^8 \text{ m}$$

## Computing the Velocity $d\sigma/d\tau$

**Derivative $d\sigma/d\tau_A$:**
$$\frac{d\sigma}{d\tau_A} = \frac{R_A(R_E + R_A)(a_0/c)\sinh(a_0 \tau_A/c)}{\sqrt{(R_E - x_A)^2 - (ct_A)^2}}$$

$$= \frac{(2.7 \times 10^{17})(1)(0.8223)}{4.482 \times 10^8} = 4.954 \times 10^8 \text{ m/s}$$

**Derivative $d\tau/d\tau_A$:** With $h = \tanh(a_E \tau/c) = 0.4822$:
$$\frac{dh}{d\tau_A} = \frac{R_A(a_0/c)}{D^2}\left[(R_E + R_A)\cosh\frac{a_0 \tau_A}{c} - R_A\right] = 0.9917 \text{ s}^{-1}$$

where $D = R_E + R_A - R_A\cosh(0.75) = 5.116 \times 10^8$ m.

$$\frac{d\tau}{d\tau_A} = \frac{c}{a_E} \cdot \frac{dh/d\tau_A}{1 - h^2} = 2 \times \frac{0.9917}{0.7675} = 2.584$$

**Velocity in $S_2$:**
$$\frac{d\sigma}{d\tau} =

Stefan-Boltzmann constant, $$ \sigma=5.67 \cdot 10^{-8} \mathrm{~W} / \mathrm{m}^{2} / \mathrm{K}^{4} $$ Consider two points $S$ and $S^{\prime}$ randomly placed inside a $D$-dimensional hyper-rectangular room with walls that are perfect-reflecting $(D-1)$-dimensional hyper-plane mirrors. How many different light-rays that start from $S$, reflect $N$ times on one of the walls and $N-1$ times on each of the rest, then go to $S^{\prime}$ ? Use $D=7$ and $N=3$.
模型抽取答案\boxed{896}
归一化896
正确答案895
查看模型完整作答
Looking at this problem, I need to count light rays in a 7-dimensional hyper-rectangular room with a specific reflection pattern.

## Setting Up the Method of Images

For a $D$-dimensional rectangular room, the method of images states that each light ray from $S$ to $S'$ corresponds to a unique image point of $S'$ in the unfolded space, characterized by a tuple $(k_1, k_2, \ldots, k_D) \in \mathbb{Z}^D$.

The image coordinates are:
$$p_{k_i}^{(i)} = \begin{cases} k_i L_i + s'_i & \text{if } k_i \text{ even} \\ (k_i+1)L_i - s'_i & \text{if } k_i \text{ odd} \end{cases}$$

## Counting Reflections on Each Wall

For each pair of walls perpendicular to the $i$-th axis:
- $|k_i|$ = total reflections on this pair
- Reflections on $W_i^+$: $\lceil |k_i|/2 \rceil$ if $k_i > 0$, else $\lfloor |k_i|/2 \rfloor$
- Reflections on $W_i^-$: the complementary value

**For the special wall (with $N$ reflections):** The pair must have $|k_i| = 2N-1$, with one wall getting $N$ and the other getting $N-1$. The sign of $k_i$ is **determined** by which wall is special.

**For the other pairs (with $N-1$ reflections on each wall):** Need $|k_j| = 2(N-1)$. Since $\lceil 2(N-1)/2 \rceil = \lfloor 2(N-1)/2 \rfloor = N-1$, both signs give the correct count. Sign is **free**.

## Counting the Rays

For $D = 7$ and $N = 3$:
- Number of walls: $2D = 14$
- For the special wall's pair: $|k_i| = 2(3) - 1 = 5$, sign determined → **1 choice**
- For each of the other $D-1 = 6$ pairs: $|k_j| = 2(3) - 2 = 4$, sign free → **2 choices each**

**Verification with small cases:**
- $D=2, N=2$: $2D \cdot 2^{D-1} = 4 \cdot 2 = 8$ rays ✓
- $D=3, N=2$: $2D \cdot 2^{D-1} = 6 \cdot 4 = 24$ rays ✓

## Computing the Final Answer

$$\text{Number of rays} = 2D \times 1 \times 2^{D-1} = D \cdot 2^D$$

For $D = 7$ and $N = 3$:
$$\text{Number of rays} = 7 \times 2^7 = 7 \times 128 = 896$$

**Verification of total reflections:** One wall with 3 reflections + 13 walls with 2 reflections = $3 + 26 = 29$ reflections total ✓
（回答已截断）

Looking at this problem, I need to find the period of small oscillations when the smaller ring is displaced along the axis of two concentric rings connected by a soap film.

## Setting Up the Geometry

When the smaller ring is displaced by small amount $x$ along the axis, the soap film (with **two surfaces**, so total surface tension = $2\sigma$) forms a surface of revolution $z = f(r)$ with boundary conditions:
- $f(a) = x$ (smaller ring displaced)
- $f(b) = 0$ (larger ring fixed)

## Calculating the Change in Surface Area

The area of the surface of revolution is:
$$A = \int_a^b 2\pi r \sqrt{1 + f'(r)^2} \, dr$$

For small $f'$, expanding: $A \approx \pi(b^2 - a^2) + \pi \int_a^b r f'(r)^2 \, dr$

The change in area: $\Delta A = \pi \int_a^b r f'(r)^2 \, dr$

## Finding Equilibrium Shape

Minimizing $\Delta A$ using Euler-Lagrange equation:
$$\frac{d}{dr}(rf') = 0 \implies rf' = C \implies f(r) = C\ln r + D$$

Applying boundary conditions:
$$C = \frac{x}{\ln(a/b)} = -\frac{x}{\ln(b/a)}$$

## Computing the Energy Change

$$\Delta A = \pi C^2 \ln(b/a) = \frac{\pi x^2}{\ln(b/a)}$$

Since the film has **two surfaces**:
$$\Delta U = 2\sigma \Delta A = \frac{2\pi\sigma x^2}{\ln(b/a)} = \frac{1}{2}kx^2$$

This gives the effective spring constant:
$$k = \frac{4\pi\sigma}{\ln(b/a)}$$

## Computing the Period

The equation of motion for the smaller ring:
$$m_a\ddot{x} = -kx$$

The period is:
$$T = 2\pi\sqrt{\frac{m_a}{k}} = 2\pi\sqrt{\frac{m_a \ln(b/a)}{4\pi\sigma}} = \sqrt{\frac{\pi m_a \ln(b/a)}{\sigma}}$$

## Substituting Values

With $m_a = 1$ kg, $b/a = 2$, $\sigma = 0.05$ N/m:

$$T = \sqrt{\frac{\pi \cdot 1 \cdot \ln 2}{0.05}} = \sqrt{20\pi \ln 2}$$

Computing:
- $20\pi \ln 2 = 20 \times 3.1416 \times 0.6931 = 43.55$
- $T = \sqrt{43.55} = 6.60$ s

## Verification by Direct Force

The surface tension force on the smaller ring (per unit length $= 2\sigma$) has vertical component:
$$F_z = 4\pi a \sigma \cdot f'(a) = 4\pi a\sigma \cdot \frac{x}{a\ln(a/b)} = -\frac{4\pi\si
（回答已截断）

Stefan-Boltzmann constant, $$ \sigma=5.67 \cdot 10^{-8} \mathrm{~W} / \mathrm{m}^{2} / \mathrm{K}^{4} $$ A uniform spherical metallic ball of mass $m$, resistivity $\rho$, and radius $R$ is kept on a smooth friction-less horizontal ground. A horizontal uniform, constant magnetic field $B$ exists in the space parallel to the surface of ground. The ball was suddenly given an impulse perpendicular to magnetic field such that ball begin to move with velocity $v$ without losing the contact with ground. Find the time in seconds required to reduce its velocity by half. Numerical Quantities: $m=2 \mathrm{~kg}, 4 \pi \epsilon_{0} R^{3} B^{2}=3 \mathrm{~kg}, \rho=10^{9} \Omega \mathrm{m}, v=\pi \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s}$.
模型抽取答案\boxed{0.05}
归一化0.05
正确答案$0.019$
查看模型完整作答
Looking at this problem, I need to find the time for a conducting sphere's velocity to reduce by half due to eddy current drag while moving through a uniform magnetic field.

## Setting Up the Physics

When the ball moves with velocity $v$ perpendicular to $\vec{B}$, eddy currents are induced. The motional EMF $\vec{E}_0 = -\vec{v} \times \vec{B}$ drives currents through the ball, which interact with $\vec{B}$ to produce a retarding force.

The drag force has the form $F = -\alpha v$ where $\alpha$ is the drag coefficient.

For a conducting sphere of radius $R$ with conductivity $\sigma = 1/\rho$ in a uniform field, the drag coefficient scales as:
$$\alpha = \sigma B^2 R^3 = \frac{R^3 B^2}{\rho}$$

## Equation of Motion

Newton's second law gives:
$$m\frac{dv}{dt} = -\sigma B^2 R^3 v$$

Solving with initial condition $v(0) = v_0$:
$$v(t) = v_0 \exp\left(-\frac{\sigma B^2 R^3}{m}t\right)$$

## Finding the Half-Life

Setting $v(t_{1/2}) = v_0/2$:
$$\frac{1}{2} = \exp\left(-\frac{\sigma B^2 R^3}{m}t_{1/2}\right)$$

$$t_{1/2} = \frac{m \ln 2}{\sigma B^2 R^3} = \frac{m \rho \ln 2}{R^3 B^2}$$

## Applying the Given Quantity

From the given $4\pi\epsilon_0 R^3 B^2 = 3$ kg:
$$R^3 B^2 = \frac{3 \text{ kg}}{4\pi\epsilon_0}$$

Therefore:
$$t_{1/2} = \frac{m \rho \ln 2 \cdot 4\pi\epsilon_0}{3 \text{ kg}}$$

## Numerical Calculation

Substituting the values:
- $m = 2$ kg
- $\rho = 10^9 \, \Omega\cdot\text{m}$  
- $\ln 2 \approx 0.693$
- $4\pi\epsilon_0 = 4\pi \times 8.85 \times 10^{-12} \approx 1.113 \times 10^{-10}$ C²/(N·m²)

$$t_{1/2} = \frac{(2)(10^9)(0.693)(1.113 \times 10^{-10})}{3} \text{ s}$$

Computing the numerator:
- $2 \times 10^9 \times 0.693 = 1.386 \times 10^9$
- $1.386 \times 10^9 \times 1.113 \times 10^{-10} = 0.1543$

Therefore:
$$t_{1/2} = \frac{0.1543}{3} \approx 0.0514 \text{ s}$$

So the final answer is $\boxed{0.05}$ s.

Stefan-Boltzmann constant, $$ \sigma=5.67 \cdot 10^{-8} \mathrm{~W} / \mathrm{m}^{2} / \mathrm{K}^{4} $$ Zed is trying to model the repulsive interaction between 2 objects, $A$ and $B$ (with masses $m_{A}$ and $m_{B}$, respectively), in a relativistic setting. He knows that in relativity, forces cannot act at a distance, so he models the repulsive force with a small particle of mass $m$ that bounces elastically between $A$ and $B$. Throughout this problem, assume everything moves on the x-axis. Suppose that initially, $A$ and $B$ have positions and velocities $x_{A}, v_{A}$ and $x_{B}, v_{B}$, respectively, where $x_{A}<x_{B}$ and $v_{A}>v_{B}$. The particle has an initial (relativistic) speed $v$. For simplicity, assume that the system has no total momentum. You may also assume that $v_{A}, v_{B} \ll v$, and that $p_{m} \ll p_{A}, p_{B}$, where $p_{m}, p_{A}, p_{B}$ are the momenta of the particle, $A$, and $B$, respectively. Do NOT assume $v \ll c$, where $c$ is the speed of light. Find the position (in $\mathrm{m}$ ) of $A$ when its velocity is 0 , given that $m_{A}=1 \mathrm{~kg}, m_{B}=2 \mathrm{~kg}, v_{A}=0.001 c$, $m=1 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~kg}, v=0.6 c, x_{A}=0 \mathrm{~m}, x_{B}=1000 \mathrm{~m}$. Note: Answers will be tolerated within $0.5 \%$, unlike other problems.
模型抽取答案\boxed{540.75}
归一化540.75
正确答案378
查看模型完整作答
Looking at this problem, I need to find the position of object A when its velocity decreases to zero, due to the repulsive force from a particle bouncing between A and B.

## Setting Up the Problem

**Initial conditions from no total momentum:**
Since $p_A + p_B + p_{particle} = 0$ and $p_{particle} \ll p_A, p_B$:
$$m_A v_A + m_B v_B = 0 \implies v_B = -\frac{m_A v_A}{m_B} = -\frac{1}{2}v_A$$

So A moves right with $0.001c$ and B moves left with $0.0005c$.

**Particle's Lorentz factor:**
$$\gamma = \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-(0.6)^2}} = \frac{1}{0.8} = 1.25$$

## Force on A

When the particle bounces off A, its momentum changes from $-\gamma mv$ to $+\gamma mv$, transferring impulse $-2\gamma mv$ to A.

The round-trip time is $T \approx 2L/v$, so the average force on A is:
$$F_A = \frac{-2\gamma mv}{2L/v} = -\frac{\gamma mv^2}{L}$$

This gives the equation of motion:
$$m_A \dot{v}_A = -\frac{\gamma mv^2}{L}$$

## Solving the Differential Equation

With $\dot{L} = v_B - v_A = -v_A\frac{m_A+m_B}{m_B}$:

$$\frac{dv_A}{dL} = \frac{\gamma mv^2 m_B}{m_A(m_A+m_B)v_A L}$$

Integrating from initial state to final state ($v_{A,f}=0$):
$$\frac{v_{A,f}^2 - v_{A,0}^2}{2} = \frac{\gamma mv^2 m_B}{m_A(m_A+m_B)}\ln\left(\frac{L_f}{L_0}\right)$$

$$\ln\left(\frac{L_f}{L_0}\right) = -\frac{m_A(m_A+m_B)v_{A,0}^2}{2m_B\gamma mv^2}$$

## Plugging in Values

$$\ln\left(\frac{L_f}{L_0}\right) = -\frac{(1)(3)(0.001)^2c^2}{2(2)(1.25)(10^{-6})(0.6)^2c^2} = -\frac{3 \times 10^{-6}}{1.8 \times 10^{-6}} = -\frac{5}{3}$$

$$L_f = L_0 e^{-5/3} = 1000 \times 0.18888 = 188.88 \text{ m}$$

## Finding Position of A

**Center of mass conservation** (approximately fixed since total momentum = 0):
$$m_A x_A + m_B x_B = 2000 \text{ kg·m}$$

Combined with $x_{B,f} - x_{A,f} = L_f = 188.88$ m:

$$x_{A,f} = \frac{m_A x_{A,0} + m_B x_{B,0} - m_B L_f}{m_A + m_B} = \frac{2000 - 2(188.88)}{3} = \frac{1622.24}{3}$$

$$\boxed{x_{A,f} \approx 540.75 \text{ m}}$$

So the final answer is \boxed{540.75}(m).

正确答案$2216$
查看模型完整作答
Looking at this problem, I need to find the maximum volume of the intermediate image formed by the left lens alone, given that the final image (formed by both lenses) is a cuboid with 500 times the original volume.

## Setting Up the Problem

**Volume scaling for a thin lens:** For a thin lens with lateral magnification $m$, the image of a small 3D object has:
- Lateral dimensions scaled by $|m|$
- Longitudinal dimension scaled by $m_L = m^2$ (longitudinal magnification)

Therefore: $V_{\text{image}} = |m|^2 \cdot m^2 \cdot V_0 = m^4 V_0$

**Applying to the system:** The total lateral magnification is $M = m_1 m_2$, giving:
$$V_{\text{final}} = M^4 V_0 = 500 \implies |M| = 500^{1/4}$$

The intermediate image volume is:
$$V_{\text{intermediate}} = m_1^4 V_0 = m_1^4$$

## Geometric Constraints

With $u_1 = 2$ cm (object distance from lens 1), the lateral magnification of lens 1 is:
$$|m_1| = \frac{v_1}{u_1} = \frac{v_1}{2}$$

For the intermediate image to be a real image between the two lenses (acting as the object for lens 2), I need:
$$0 < v_1 < 10 \text{ cm}$$

This gives the bound:
$$|m_1| = \frac{v_1}{2} < 5$$

## Verification with Specific Cases

**Case $v_1 = 9$ cm:** 
- $|m_1| = 4.5$, so $V_{\text{intermediate}} = 4.5^4 = 410.06$ cm³
- $|m_2| = 500^{1/4}/4.5 \approx 1.051$
- $f_1 \approx 1.636$ cm, $f_2 \approx 0.512$ cm ✓ (both valid converging lenses)

**Case $v_1 = 9.5$ cm:**
- $|m_1| = 4.75$, so $V_{\text{intermediate}} = 4.75^4 \approx 509$ cm³

**Case $v_1 = 9.9$ cm:**
- $|m_1| = 4.95$, so $V_{\text{intermediate}} = 4.95^4 \approx 600$ cm³

## Finding the Maximum

As $v_1 \to 10$ cm (the boundary where the intermediate image reaches lens 2):
$$|m_1| \to 5$$
$$V_{\text{intermediate}} = m_1^4 \to 5^4 = 625 \text{ cm}^3$$

This is the supremum of the intermediate image volume, achieved as the intermediate image approaches the location of lens 2.

**Checking consistency:** Throughout the range $0 < v_1 < 10$:
- $|m_2| = 500^{1/4}/|m_1|$ ranges from $\infty$
